                                                                           OPEC+ crude output rose by 1.61 million barrels/day month
    European diesel market participants expect little relief to the    over month in September, led by a recovery in Saudi Arabia, the
global diesel balance from US President Donald Trump's plan to         latest Platts OPEC+ survey from S&P Global Energy found.
import Russian diesel, doubting it will ease tightness or weigh
on prices.                                                                 OPEC+ produced 35.27 million b/d in September, well below
                                                                       levels of 42.89 million b/d in February, before the US and Israel
    Trump said Oct. 9 that Russia would supply additional diesel       attacked Iran, triggering attacks on energy infrastructure and
to the market.                                                         severe shipping restrictions in the region. The output fall is also
                                                                       partially due to the UAE leaving OPEC in May.
    "I have just concluded a highly successful discussion with
President Vladimir Putin of Russia, wherein it was agreed                  September output was at a wartime high, as some producers
that Russia will immediately supply over 300,000 Tons of               in the Middle East exported more crude.
Diesel Fuel to the American and Global Marketplace, another
500,000 Tons during the month of November, and 1,000,000
Tons immediately thereafter," Trump said on his Truth Social
media account.

    "Additionally, based on the condition of their Diesel Refineries,
Russia will then deliver, within a short period of time, 3,000,000
Tons of Diesel Fuel," Trump said.

    A European diesel trader said: "The flat price did respond a
little; however, it is a relatively small quantity versus what Russia
used to export, and it largely comes without a timeline, so the
market is lacking clarity."

    Another Europe-based market source said: "We do not see it
moving anything materially. It is just a readjustment of flows ...
Russia will not have a surplus to export."

(continued on page 15)

                                                                           OPEC output rose by 1.58 million b/d month over month
                                                                       to 21.24 million b/d, led by a recovery in Saudi Arabia, which
                                                                       produced 8 million b/d, up 1.65 million b/d month over month, the
                                                                       survey showed.

                                                                           Iraq also contributed to the rise, boosting production by
                                                                       200,000 b/d to 3.9 million b/d. This was due to a 25% increase
                                                                       in exports, as more Iraqi oil passed through the Strait of Hormuz
                                                                       and Ceyhan on Turkey's Mediterranean coast.

                                                                           Non-OPEC allies increased production by 20,000 b/d to 14.03
                                                                       million b/d in September, as Russia managed to hold output
                                                                       steady after months of cuts linked to multiple attacks on its
                                                                       refining infrastructure.

                                                                       US rejoins International Energy Forum

                                                                           Saudi Arabia plans to temporarily convert a gas pipeline to
                                                                       transport additional oil to help address disruptions to its export
                                                                       capacity due to the conflict in the Middle East, energy minister
                                                                       Prince Abdulaziz bin Salman said Oct. 12.

                                                                           Bin Salman said that an unspecified gas pipeline could
                                                                       be retrofitted with pumping stations and compressors to
                                                                       accommodate oil flows and provide an estimated 1.2 million b/d
                                                                       of capacity within seven to eight weeks.

                                                                           The pipeline would be lent to the Saudi Arabian Oil Co. until
                                                                       "May", then returned for use as a gas pipeline ahead of the peak-
                                                                       demand summer season, he said without specifying a year while
                                                                       speaking at the 25th WPC Energy Congress in Riyadh.

                                                                           "That pipeline, with some more investment, we will take it
                                                                       up to 3 million [barrels per day]," bin Salman said. Alongside
                                                                       a planned improvement to the East-West Pipeline's pumping
                                                                       stations, Saudi Arabia could reach 10 million b/d in pipeline
                                                                       transportation flows within two to three years, he added.

Master Gas
    Saudi Aramco's Master Gas System has operated "reliably"

through the Iran war, connecting energy resources with electricity
generation, desalination, refining, petrochemicals, and industry,
Nasser said. Saudi Arabia is targeting roughly an 80% increase in
sales/gas production capacity by 2030 versus 2021 production
levels, according to Nasser.

    "We are also exploring future opportunities in hydrogen"
alongside carbon capture, utilization, and storage and "circular
carbon technologies," Nasser said.

    The country is also building solar and wind power capacity to
diversify electricity supplies and help to lower emissions, he said.
Gas and renewables will provide the country's electricity in equal
shares by 2030, according to the statement.

    The company's refineries are also providing gasoline, diesel,
jet fuel, asphalt, and industrial feedstocks to the domestic
economy, while petrochemicals are supporting Saudi Arabia's
manufacturing, he said.
